The spin-off games _[Lego Harry Potter: Years 1–4](/wiki/Lego_Harry_Potter:_Years_1%E2%80%934 "Lego Harry Potter: Years 1–4")_ and _[Lego Harry Potter: Years 5–7](/wiki/Lego_Harry_Potter:_Years_5%E2%80%937 "Lego Harry Potter: Years 5–7")_ were developed by [Traveller's Tales](/wiki/Traveller%27s_Tales "Traveller's Tales") and published by [Warner Bros. Interactive Entertainment](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Interactive_Entertainment "Warner Bros. Interactive Entertainment"). The spin-off games _[Book of Spells](/wiki/Book_of_Spells "Book of Spells")_ and _[Book of Potions](/wiki/Book_of_Potions "Book of Potions")_ were developed by [London Studio](/wiki/London_Studio "London Studio") and use the [Wonderbook](/wiki/Wonderbook "Wonderbook"), an [augmented reality](/wiki/Augmented_reality "Augmented reality") book designed to be used in conjunction with the [PlayStation Move](/wiki/PlayStation_Move "PlayStation Move") and [PlayStation Eye](/wiki/PlayStation_Eye "PlayStation Eye").[262] The _Harry Potter_ universe, the Wizarding World, is also featured in _[Lego Dimensions](/wiki/Lego_Dimensions "Lego Dimensions")_ , with the settings and side characters featured in the Harry Potter Adventure World, and Harry, Voldemort, and Hermione as playable characters. In 2017, [Warner Bros. Interactive Entertainment](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Interactive_Entertainment "Warner Bros. Interactive Entertainment") opened its own Harry Potter-themed game design studio, by the name of [Portkey Games](/wiki/Portkey_Games "Portkey Games"), before releasing _[Hogwarts Mystery](/wiki/Harry_Potter:_Hogwarts_Mystery "Harry Potter: Hogwarts Mystery")_ , developed by Jam City, in 2018 and _[Hogwarts Legacy](/wiki/Hogwarts_Legacy "Hogwarts Legacy")_ , developed by [Avalanche Software](/wiki/Avalanche_Software "Avalanche Software"), in 2023.[263][264]

### Stage production

Main article: [Harry Potter and the Cursed Child](/wiki/Harry_Potter_and_the_Cursed_Child "Harry Potter and the Cursed Child")

[](/wiki/File:Palace_Theatre_at_Dusk_2.jpg)_[Harry Potter and the Cursed Child](/wiki/Harry_Potter_and_the_Cursed_Child "Harry Potter and the Cursed Child")_ playing at the West End's [Palace Theatre](/wiki/Palace_Theatre,_London "Palace Theatre, London")

_Harry Potter and the Cursed Child: Parts I and II_ is a play which serves as a sequel to the books, beginning nineteen years after the events of _[Harry Potter and the Deathly Hallows](/wiki/Harry_Potter_and_the_Deathly_Hallows "Harry Potter and the Deathly Hallows")_. It was written by [Jack Thorne](/wiki/Jack_Thorne_\(writer\) "Jack Thorne \(writer\)") based on an original new story by Thorne, Rowling and [John Tiffany](/wiki/John_Tiffany "John Tiffany").[265] It has run at the [Palace Theatre](/wiki/Palace_Theatre,_London "Palace Theatre, London") in London's [West End](/wiki/West_End_theatre "West End theatre") since previews began on 7 June 2016 with an official premiere on 30 June 2016.[266] The first four months of tickets for the June–September performances were sold out within several hours upon release.[267] Forthcoming productions are planned for Broadway[268] and Melbourne.[269]

The script was released as a book at the time of the premiere, with a revised version following the next year. 

### Television

Main article: [_Harry Potter_ (TV series)](/wiki/Harry_Potter_\(TV_series\) "Harry Potter \(TV series\)")

On 25 January 2021, it was reported that a live-action television series has been in early development at [HBO Max](/wiki/HBO_Max "HBO Max"). Though it was noted that the series has "complicated rights issues", due to a seven-year rights deal with Warner Bros. Domestic TV Distribution that included US broadcast, cable and streaming rights to the franchise, which ended in April 2025.[270] On 12 April 2023, the series was confirmed to be in development, and will be streamed on the new streaming service [HBO Max](/wiki/HBO_Max "HBO Max").[271][272][273] On 23 February 2024, [Warner Bros. Discovery](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Discovery "Warner Bros. Discovery") CEO [David Zaslav](/wiki/David_Zaslav "David Zaslav") announced that the series would debut on Max in 2026. On 25 June 2024, it was announced the series was moved from HBO Max to [HBO](/wiki/HBO "HBO").[274] The series is planned to adapt one book per season, with seven seasons planned.[275]

An open casting call for the three leading young actors in the UK and Ireland was announced in September 2024, with _Variety_ reporting that 32,000 children auditioned for the roles, with filming to start in summer 2025.[276][277][278] In November 2024, it was reported that [Mark Rylance](/wiki/Mark_Rylance "Mark Rylance") was the front runner to play Dumbledore in the series.[279] On 25 February 2025, American actor [John Lithgow](/wiki/John_Lithgow "John Lithgow") confirmed reports that he had been cast as Dumbledore in the series.[280][281] He acknowledged there would be controversy over him playing the role as an American, when the film series only cast British or Irish cast members at Rowling's request, and that he would work with a dialect coach. He also suggested filming would start in August after he finished portraying Roald Dahl on stage in London.[282] He said "I don't know the Harry Potter canon by heart as 98% of the world's population seems to," but said that in talks with the producers he was intrigued by how "as the kids grew older [in the books], you learn more and more about Dumbledore, and he became a much more surprising, complicated character."[283] He also said he had started reading the books in preparation.[284]

News reports also speculated in March 2025 that a casting notice seeking children as extras near [Warner Bros. Studios Leavesden](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Studios_Leavesden "Warner Bros. Studios Leavesden") was for the new series.[285][286] On 7 March 2025, it was reported that English actor [Paapa Essiedu](/wiki/Paapa_Essiedu "Paapa Essiedu") and English actress [Janet McTeer](/wiki/Janet_McTeer "Janet McTeer") were in negotiations to portray [Severus Snape](/wiki/Severus_Snape "Severus Snape") and [Minerva McGonagall](/wiki/Minerva_McGonagall "Minerva McGonagall") respectively in the series.[287] On 27 May 2025, it was announced that [Dominic McLaughlin](/wiki/Dominic_McLaughlin "Dominic McLaughlin"), [Alastair Stout](/wiki/Alastair_Stout "Alastair Stout") and [Arabella Stanton](/wiki/Arabella_Stanton "Arabella Stanton") were cast as [Harry Potter](/wiki/Harry_Potter_\(character\) "Harry Potter \(character\)"), [Ron Weasley](/wiki/Ron_Weasley "Ron Weasley") and [Hermione Granger](/wiki/Hermione_Granger "Hermione Granger") respectively.[288] On 9 June 2025, it was revealed that [Bel Powley](/wiki/Bel_Powley "Bel Powley") would play Petunia Dursley and [Daniel Rigby](/wiki/Daniel_Rigby "Daniel Rigby") would play Vernon Dursley.[289] It was also revealed that [Lox Pratt](/wiki/Lox_Pratt "Lox Pratt") would play Draco Malfoy, [Johnny Flynn](/wiki/Johnny_Flynn "Johnny Flynn") would play Lucius Malfoy, [Katherine Parkinson](/wiki/Katherine_Parkinson "Katherine Parkinson") would play Molly Weasley, Leo Earley would play Seamus Finnigan, Alessia Leoni would play Parvati Patil, Sienna Moosah would play Lavender Brown, and [Bertie Carvel](/wiki/Bertie_Carvel "Bertie Carvel") would play Cornelius Fudge.[290]

### Full-cast audiobooks

In 2024, it was announced that the novels would be adapted into full-cast audio productions with a voice cast of over 100 [voice actors](/wiki/Voice_actor "Voice actor") to be released in late 2025.[291] In August 2025, it was announced that the audio dramas would be released monthly, starting on 4 November 2025.[292]

## Attractions

Main articles: [The Wizarding World of Harry Potter](/wiki/The_Wizarding_World_of_Harry_Potter "The Wizarding World of Harry Potter") and [Warner Bros. Studio Tour London – The Making of Harry Potter](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Studio_Tour_London_%E2%80%93_The_Making_of_Harry_Potter "Warner Bros. Studio Tour London – The Making of Harry Potter")

[](/wiki/File:Wizarding_World_of_Harry_Potter_Castle.jpg)Hogwarts Castle as depicted in the Wizarding World of Harry Potter, located in Universal Orlando Resort's Island of Adventure

Universal and Warner Bros. created _The Wizarding World of Harry Potter_ , a _Harry Potter_ -themed expansion to the [Islands of Adventure](/wiki/Islands_of_Adventure "Islands of Adventure") theme park at [Universal Orlando Resort](/wiki/Universal_Orlando_Resort "Universal Orlando Resort") in Florida. It opened to the public on 18 June 2010.[297] It includes a recreation of [Hogsmeade](/wiki/Hogsmeade "Hogsmeade") and several rides; its flagship attraction is _[Harry Potter and the Forbidden Journey](/wiki/Harry_Potter_and_the_Forbidden_Journey "Harry Potter and the Forbidden Journey")_ , which exists within a recreation of [Hogwarts](/wiki/Hogwarts "Hogwarts") School of Witchcraft and Wizardry.[298]

In 2014 Universal opened a _Harry Potter_ -themed area at the [Universal Studios Florida](/wiki/Universal_Studios_Florida "Universal Studios Florida") theme park. It includes a recreation of [Diagon Alley](/wiki/Diagon_Alley "Diagon Alley").[299] The flagship attraction is the _[Harry Potter and the Escape from Gringotts](/wiki/Harry_Potter_and_the_Escape_from_Gringotts "Harry Potter and the Escape from Gringotts")_ roller coaster ride.[300] A completely functioning [full-scale replica of the Hogwarts Express](/wiki/Hogwarts_Express_\(Universal_Orlando_Resort\) "Hogwarts Express \(Universal Orlando Resort\)") was created for the Diagon Alley expansion, connecting King's Cross Station at Universal Studios to the Hogsmeade station at Islands of Adventure.[301][302] _The Wizarding World of Harry Potter_ opened at the [Universal Studios Hollywood](/wiki/Universal_Studios_Hollywood "Universal Studios Hollywood") theme park near [Los Angeles, California](/wiki/Los_Angeles,_California "Los Angeles, California") in 2016,[303][304] and in [Universal Studios Japan](/wiki/Universal_Studios_Japan "Universal Studios Japan") theme park in [Osaka](/wiki/Osaka "Osaka"), Japan in 2014. The Osaka venue includes the village of Hogsmeade, _Harry Potter and the Forbidden Journey_ ride, and _[Flight of the Hippogriff](/wiki/Flight_of_the_Hippogriff "Flight of the Hippogriff")_ roller coaster.[305][306] Other _Harry Potter_ roller coasters are the _[Dragon Challenge](/wiki/Dragon_Challenge "Dragon Challenge")_ which closed to make way for _[Hagrid's Magical Creatures Motorbike Adventure](/wiki/Hagrid%27s_Magical_Creatures_Motorbike_Adventure "Hagrid's Magical Creatures Motorbike Adventure")_ , at [Universal Islands of Adventure](/wiki/Universal_Islands_of_Adventure "Universal Islands of Adventure"). 

[Warner Bros. Studio Tour London – The Making of _Harry Potter_](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Studio_Tour_London_%E2%80%93_The_Making_of_Harry_Potter "Warner Bros. Studio Tour London – The Making of Harry Potter") is a behind-the-scenes walking tour in London featuring authentic sets, costumes and props from the film series. The attraction is located at [Warner Bros. Studios, Leavesden](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Studios_Leavesden "Warner Bros. Studios Leavesden"), where all eight of the _Harry Potter_ films were made. Warner Bros. constructed two new sound stages to house and showcase the sets from each of the British-made productions, following a £100 million investment.[307] It opened to the public in March 2012.[308] A [Japan based version of the studio tour](/wiki/Warner_Bros._Studio_Tours#Warner_Bros._Studio_Tour_Tokyo_–_The_Making_of_Harry_Potter "Warner Bros. Studio Tours") was announced in August 2020 and opened on 16 June 2023.[309]

## Supplementary works

See also: [J. K. Rowling § Philanthropy](/wiki/J._K._Rowling#Philanthropy "J. K. Rowling")

Rowling expanded the [_Harry Potter_ universe](/wiki/Harry_Potter_universe "Harry Potter universe") with short books produced for charities.[310][311] In 2001, she released _[Fantastic Beasts and Where to Find Them](/wiki/Fantastic_Beasts_and_Where_to_Find_Them_\(book\) "Fantastic Beasts and Where to Find Them \(book\)")_ (a purported Hogwarts textbook) and _[Quidditch Through the Ages](/wiki/Quidditch_Through_the_Ages "Quidditch Through the Ages")_ (a book Harry reads for fun). Proceeds from the sale of these two books benefited the charity [Comic Relief](/wiki/Comic_Relief "Comic Relief").[312] In 2007, Rowling composed seven handwritten copies of _[The Tales of Beedle the Bard](/wiki/The_Tales_of_Beedle_the_Bard "The Tales of Beedle the Bard")_ , a collection of fairy tales that is featured in the final novel, one of which was auctioned to raise money for the Children's High Level Group, a fund for mentally disabled children in poor countries. The book was published internationally on 4 December 2008.[313][314] Rowling also wrote an 800-word [prequel](/wiki/Harry_Potter_prequel "Harry Potter prequel") in 2008 as part of a fundraiser organised by the bookseller [Waterstones](/wiki/Waterstones "Waterstones").[315] All three of these books contain extra information about the wizarding world not included in the original novels. 

In 2016, she released three new e-books: _[Hogwarts: An Incomplete and Unreliable Guide](/wiki/Hogwarts:_An_Incomplete_and_Unreliable_Guide "Hogwarts: An Incomplete and Unreliable Guide")_ , _[Short Stories from Hogwarts of Power, Politics and Pesky Poltergeists](/wiki/Short_Stories_from_Hogwarts_of_Power,_Politics_and_Pesky_Poltergeists "Short Stories from Hogwarts of Power, Politics and Pesky Poltergeists")_ and _[Short Stories from Hogwarts of Heroism, Hardship and Dangerous Hobbies](/wiki/Short_Stories_from_Hogwarts_of_Heroism,_Hardship_and_Dangerous_Hobbies "Short Stories from Hogwarts of Heroism, Hardship and Dangerous Hobbies")_.[316]

Rowling's website [Pottermore](/wiki/Pottermore "Pottermore") was launched in 2012.[317] Pottermore allows users to be sorted, be chosen by their wand and play various minigames. The main purpose of the website was to allow the user to journey through the story with access to content not revealed by JK Rowling previously, with over 18,000 words of additional content.[318] The site was redesigned in 2015 as WizardingWorld and it mainly focuses on the information already available, rather than exploration.[319]
